This chapter describes sacred places, Kāmarūpa's boundaries, and Kāmakhyā's yoni-pīṭha. River and place names are retained without forcing uncertain modern identifications. Claims of ritual efficacy are the source's. This is a provisional reading translation.

11.1 — The Goddess said:
You are worshipped by gods, Dānavas, Gandharvas, and the lord of gods; honoured by Gaṇeśa, Nandi, Candreśa, Govinda, and the Creator—

11.2
—your feet honoured by the greatest yogins, teacher of all worlds, Hara. [Concerning] the supreme Vidyā Kālī, destroyer of impurity, whom you described—

11.3
—her mantras, practice, worship, preliminary observance, gestures, bali offering, fire-offering, disposition, and place—

11.4
—meditation, hymn, and armour: I have heard them before. Now, Great Lord, I wish to hear the distinctions between places.

11.5
Where is liberation obtained? Where is excellent accomplishment obtained swiftly? Mahādeva, Śaṅkara, graciously tell me.

11.6
Supreme Lord, [describe the place] by resorting to which people quickly obtain the fruits of their own undertakings without much exertion.

11.7 — Īśvara said:
Goddess, listen: I shall describe the exceedingly rare place, bringing swift accomplishment and bestowing the fruit of great liberation.

11.8
For Kālikā no place is praised above the cremation ground. Whatever rite is performed there yields an infinite fruit.

11.9
If even one preliminary mantra observance is performed there, Supreme Lady, with the proper disposition and Śakti, Great Goddess, [nothing equals it]—

Note: The central clause is damaged; the bracketed sense follows the Hindi provisionally. Saśaktika can mean accompanied by Śakti or a ritual partner, rather than merely “devoted.”

11.10
—accomplishment of the mantra certainly arises; there is nothing surprising in this. Worship bestows an infinite fruit everywhere, in water or on land—

11.11
—if it is done with divine or heroic disposition, Goddess. Whether Śākta, Vaiṣṇava, Śaiva, or otherwise—

11.12
—one who repeats in Vārāṇasī for a single month, beautiful-faced one, beginning in the morning and continuing until midday—

11.13
—certainly attains accomplishment of the mantra: this is true, giver of excellent accomplishment. Queen of the gods, auspicious one, that person certainly attains nirvāṇa.

11.14
Queen of the gods, [its names include] Mahāśmaśāna, Ānandakānana, Avimukta, Great Goddess, and Gaurīmukha—

Note: The Sanskrit extraction gives Gaurīmukha; the Hindi instead gives Gaurīkānana. The former is retained with the variant recorded.

11.15
—Vārāṇasī, the great sacred field, the form of Kālī, higher than the highest. How could I tell you [the fruit of] whatever is done there, Goddess?

11.16
Great worship in Kāmarūpa bestows the fruits of every accomplishment. [Its extent reaches] Nepal's Kāñcana mountain [and] the confluence of the Brahmaputra—

Textual caution: The geographic clause is compressed and not a complete survey statement by itself. It is continued and qualified by the more explicit directions in17–18.

11.17
—from the Karatoyā as far as Dikkaravāsinī. To the north is Kañjagiri; the Karatoyā is to the west.

11.18
Daughter of the mountain, to the east is the foremost sacred river Dikṣu; to the south, as far as the confluence of the Brahmaputra and Lākṣā. This is renowned as Kāmarūpa, established in all the scriptures.

Note: Girikanyake is a vocative, “daughter of the mountain,” addressing the Goddess. It is not translated as a separate eastern boundary. The Hindi gloss appears to misread this construction. Kañjagiri, Dikṣu, and Lākṣā retain the edition's forms; their modern identifications require a separate historical argument.

11.19
Whoever's mind is inclined to discharge the three debts should go with supreme devotion to the presence of Kāmakhyā's yoni.

Note: The traditional three debts are to the gods, seers, and ancestors; these are explained by the Hindi but not enumerated in this verse.

11.20
Whoever goes there on pilgrimage obtains at each step the fruit of a horse sacrifice.

11.21
Know excellent Kāmarūpa as triangular, thirty yojanas broad and a hundred yojanas long.

11.22
To the northeast is Kedāra, to the northwest Gajaśāsana; to the south, Goddess, is the confluence of Lākṣā and Brahmaretas.

Note: Brahmaretas, “Brahmā's seed,” names the Brahmaputra here. Yojana is retained as a traditional distance unit, not converted into a single asserted modern measurement.

11.23
Know the triangle thus, honoured by gods and antigods. The human beings there are gods: there is no doubt.

11.24
Whatever water is there, Goddess, is a sacred ford. [Its divisions are] upavīthi, vīthi, upapīṭha, and pīṭha—

11.25
—siddhapīṭha, mahāpīṭha, brahmapīṭha, and within it viṣṇupīṭha, Great Goddess, and rudrapīṭha within that.

11.26
They are renowned as the nine yonis, situated all around in the four directions. Great worship there yields progressively greater fruits.

Note: These nine categories belong to the sacred geography of this passage; they should not be silently replaced by the separate territorial scheme of four pīṭhas.

11.27
Auspicious one, the fruit is certainly doubled and doubled in this order. Śāmbhavī, for all Vidyās and every mantra—

11.28
—in worship and repetition the fruit is doubled and doubled. Kāmakhyā's yoni-circle is designated the nine yonis.

11.29
From the yoni to Mahī, from Vidyā to Gandhamādana: Goddess, this five-krośa [region] is difficult for everyone to obtain.

Textual caution: The terms Mahī and Vidyā may be place names or damaged readings. The Hindi repeats them without resolving their topography. A precise mapped boundary is not invented here.

11.30
It is supremely wondrous, attended by Brahmā, Viṣṇu, and the lord of gods. Even gods desire to die there; how much more human beings!

11.31
Great Lady, auspicious one, those who go to the yoni-pīṭha, measuring five krośas, take Śiva's form; those who die there are not born again.

11.32
They never suffer affliction caused by the son of the sun. Those excellent people who dwell sinlessly at the yoni-pīṭha become Śaṅkaras, three-eyed and moon-crested.

Note: The Hindi identifies the sun's son as Śani. Elsewhere the epithet can denote Yama; the Sanskrit's proper identification here is not independently established.

11.33
Sovereign Goddess, the excellent practitioner who worships and repeats all Vidyās and all mantras becomes a receptacle of the eight accomplishments beginning with aṇimā.

11.34
Within it, Great Goddess, is the brilliant mountain named Nīla, formed of Brahmā, Viṣṇu, and Śiva, and consisting of all powers.

11.35
Within it, Supreme Lady, is the supreme Manobhava cave. Within that cave, [the Goddess] takes the form of red water—

11.36
—Kāmakhyā, the wish-fulfilling creeper, surrounded by ten million liṅgas. The Manobhava cave always blazes with her radiance.

11.37
Merely by touching her, iron becomes gold. Daughter of the mountain, [her extent is] four hand-lengths on every side.

11.38
Merely by touching her, a human being reaches Śiva's state and immediately becomes free of sin, Goddess: there is no doubt.

11.39
Whatever rite is performed here yields an infinite fruit.

11.40
Within it, Supreme Lady, is a circle of water, twelve finger-breadths across, surging up from the underworld.

11.41
Supreme Lady, that water has the nature of Brahmā, Viṣṇu, and Śiva. Goddess, that divine water is named the causal ocean.

11.42
What need is there for much speech, Goddess? That water is supreme nectar. Beautiful one, I have spoken hesitantly [or under constraint]; know this.

Note: The final phrase is uncertain. The Hindi reads śaṅkita, hesitation or apprehension. It is not expanded into a conjectural reason for secrecy.

11.43
Goddess, the whole moving and unmoving world continually desires that water. Merely by touching that water and that pool—

11.44
—a person immediately becomes a god, Goddess, certainly; released from merit and sin, that person is surely liberated while living.

11.45
Whoever worships in that pool with its water, Great Lady—with millions upon thousands of tongues and hundreds of millions of mouths—

11.46
—I could not describe the fruit, daughter of the mountain. Placing a hand in the pool's water, Great Lady—

11.47
—and repeating one hundred and eight times, one becomes a great lord of accomplishments on earth; the mantra is immediately accomplished, without doubt.

11.48
Whether Śaiva, Vaiṣṇava, Śākta, or another [practitioner], Great Lady, the mantra repeated there attains accomplishment at once, even through one hundred and eight repetitions. There is no doubt.

Note: The extracted Sanskrit appears to read nānya, “not another,” whereas the Hindi reads “or any other.” The inclusion of “another” in this draft follows the Hindi and context provisionally; the negation needs collation.

11.49
Whoever offers [that water] to the ancestors, lifting it with the tip of kuśa grass, thereby [obtains the fruit of] śrāddha at Gayā for a niyuta of years, Great Lady.

Note: Niyuta is a traditional large-number term; the Hindi gives one hundred thousand here. Its value is not treated as invariant across all sources.

11.50
Goddess, thus I have briefly described Kāmakhyā's yoni-circle. Great Lady, I shall explain its particular greatness.

11.51
What can be said of its greatness, glorious Goddess? There Kālī, the deliverer, dwells with millions of Yoginīs.

11.52
Chinnamastā and Bhairavī, divided sevenfold and sevenfold; Dhūmā, Bhuvaneśānī, Mātaṅgī, and Kamalālayā—

11.53
—Bhagaklinnā, Bhagadhārā, Bhagandarī, Durgā, Jayadurgā, and Mahiṣamardinī—

11.54
—and all those called subsidiary Vidyās: with all of them, and with Brahmā, Viṣṇu, Maheśa, and the others, Mahākālī always dwells there.

11.55
The pīṭha resting on Brahmā's mouth has Ugratārā as its presiding deity. Great Lady, that pīṭha is declared twofold: hidden and manifest.

11.56
The hidden is holier than the manifest and difficult even for excellent practitioners to reach. Goddess, those skilled in the two Kulas can find it everywhere.

Note: “Two Kulas” is interpreted as divine and heroic dispositions in the Hindi. That explanation is noted, not substituted for the literal expression.

11.57
At the front of the Manobhava cave, the Goddess's summit rises. That exceedingly rare pīṭha is renowned as Mahogra.

Note: The Sanskrit reads agre, “in front,” where the Hindi extraction appears to say “in the fire.” The former is followed.

11.58
Siddhikālī, whose form is Brahman, the deity Bhuvaneśvarī, dwells there: the Kālī who destroyed the Daitya Ghora.

11.59
Sitting upon that pīṭha, repeat the mantra ten times. Then the mantra becomes purified, and in that very body one becomes Śiva.

Note: The Hindi instead says at the body's end. Taddehena supports “with that body”; the difference is preserved.

11.60
Beautiful-eyed one, the fruit I described within the pool is less by a quarter; it is complete through Śiva's repetition and worship.

Textual caution: The comparison's referents are compressed. Pādahīna ordinarily means deficient by one quarter; a definitive hierarchy of the sites should not be constructed from this verse without collation.

11.61
At Jālandhara know half, and at Uḍḍiyāna half of that. In Nepal, on the [specified] lunar-day observance, mantra accomplishment certainly arises—

11.62
—near Nīlakaṇṭha; there is no doubt. Queen of gods, I have gladly explained [attainment] by repetition.

Note: Tithivāsara is not sufficiently specified to assign an exact date or duration here.

11.63
In thirteen days comes great accomplishment at Ekāmrakānana; at the Gaṅgā, through one million repetitions, accomplishment is certain, auspicious one.

11.64
In Rāḍhā and at Vikaṭākṣā, daughter of the mountain, accomplishment arises swiftly through three hundred thousand repetitions. This is true, giver of excellent accomplishment.

11.65
At Puṣkara through one hundred thousand, at Prayāga through two million; on Droṇagiri through ten million repetitions, and at Jvālā through two hundred thousand—

11.66
—likewise at Viraja through twelve hundred thousand, auspicious one; in the Himalaya through three hundred thousand, at Kedāra through five hundred thousand—

11.67
—at Kailāsa through one million, at Jayantī through five hundred thousand; at Ujjayinī in ten days, and on Mandarācala in a month—

11.68
—mantra accomplishment certainly arises through repetition and worship, auspicious one.

11.69
Daughter of the mountain, thus I have explained what you asked. Goddess, conceal it always as [one conceals] a mother's lover.

Note: The final comparison is explicit and deliberately intimate; it is not replaced by a different analogy.

COLOPHON
Thus ends the eleventh chapter, with the vernacular commentary, in the dialogue of the Goddess and Īśvara in the illustrious Yoginī Tantra, traditionally described as comprising twenty-four thousand [verses].

EDITORIAL STATUS
All69 numbered units have draft entries. Unclear readings and differences from the Hindi are marked. No independent scholarly review or definitive modern geographic identification is claimed.

============================================================================

YOGINĪ TANTRA — FIRST PART, CHAPTER 12
English working translation
Source: Mishra edition (1984), printed pp.141–155, PDF pp.161–175. Sanskrit translated with the Hindi gloss consulted. Prophetic chronology, ethnic labels and supernatural events are the text's claims. Encoded dates and damaged names are retained as uncertain rather than converted into unsupported historical identifications.

12.1 — The Goddess said:
Master, supreme bliss, giver of fearlessness to yogins, auspicious one: the greatness of Kāmākhyā's yoni which you told me—
12.2
—is true, without doubt, and entirely wondrous. I have understood its reality. Why does it not grant accomplishment in the Kali age?
12.3
I do not see knowledge of Śiva or accomplishment of mantra. Compassionate Lord, tell me the reason.
12.4 — Īśvara said:
There was a lord of the Dānavas named Narakāsura, arising from Viṣṇu's seed and born from Earth's womb.
12.5
At Earth's request Viṣṇu gave him the greatly fruitful kingdom of Kāmarūpa. That Dānava, irresistible in battle—
12.6
—defeated [the land's] Kirātas in battle and became ruler of Kāmarūpa. Again the blessed Lord joyfully gave him a dwelling—
12.7
—the yoni-region of Kāmākhyā, renowned as Prāgjyotiṣapura. When he had received royal consecration, Viṣṇu also gave him power.
12.8
Then Hari showed him the Manobhava cave; having bathed, Naraka was made free of poison [or impurity].
Note: The final verb is imperfectly recognized; “free of sin” is the Hindi's paraphrase, while nirviṣa suggests freedom from poison.
12.9 — Viṣṇu said:
Kāmākhyā's greatness accords with the meaning of every Veda. Brahmā attained Brahmāhood, and I Viṣṇuhood—
12.10
—and Śiva Śivahood through Kāmākhyā's grace. Therefore this yoni must be carefully worshipped with one's whole being.
12.11
When the Mother is gracious toward you, every prosperity is yours. When she turns away, your welfare is certainly lost.
12.12
Then I too shall abandon you, although I know you as my son. Knowing this, worship her; why should I say more?
12.13 — Īśvara continued:
Having spoken thus, Viṣṇu went to his own dwelling, Vaikuṇṭha. Naraka observed everything Viṣṇu had instructed.
12.14
Goddess, hear the terrible event that occurred at this time.
12.15
Vasiṣṭha, Brahmā's mind-born son, a sage of excellent discipline, worshipped Tārā on Nīlācala.
12.16
One day, Goddess, he came to the city's gate to worship Tārā, Queen of the gods, within Kāmākhyā's region. Naraka stopped that son of Brahmā there.
12.17 — Naraka said:
“Brahmin, wait here now; do not enter the enclosure. The Goddess is being worshipped; you may go when worship is finished.”
12.18
Hearing Naraka's words, the great sage became overwhelmed by anger, radiant as twelve suns. Red-eyed Vasiṣṭha said to Naraka:
12.19 — Vasiṣṭha said:
“Most sinful one, what have you said, foolish man? Am I unfit to enter the inner dwelling at the time of Kāmākhyā's worship—
12.20
“—to enter the yoni, fool, although born of Brahmā? Now behold my great power that will destroy you!”
12.21
Having said this, Vasiṣṭha took water in his hand from his ascetic vessel and terribly cursed the Great Goddess:
12.22
“Mother Kāmākhyā, I am a Brahmin, born of Brahmā. Today I leave you, because you have treated me otherwise. Truly, today the sin arising from killing a Brahmin shall be yours.
12.23
“By my declaration, accomplishment shall never arise here in this great seat through repetition or worship.”
12.24 — Īśvara continued:
Giving this terrible curse and casting away the water, the sage left Nīlācala and went to Mount Khāṇḍava.
12.25
Then supreme Vidyā Kāmākhyā, honoured by the world, the Goddess made of great light, whose nature is all illumination—
12.26
—who shines day and night with her own body's radiance, [burned or scorched the place] at once and went to the Kailāsa dwelling.
Note: The object of the burning verb is omitted or damaged; the Hindi also leaves its relationship unclear.
12.27
Supreme Lady, because of separation from her, the Manobhava cave was covered with masses of darkness.
12.28
Lamentation filled all worlds and the Dānava lord fainted. Seeing that supreme Māyā with sorrowful face—
12.29
—I respectfully questioned Kāmākhyā: “Auspicious one, why have you come, abandoning that yoni-region?
12.30
“Kāmākhyā, tell me why your face is sorrowful. Goddess, supremely worthy of worship, dwell in my heart. I shall remedy everything: why are you distressed?”
12.31 — Kāmākhyā said:
“Lord, the Brahmin sage Vasiṣṭha, Brahmā's son, came to my gate in the yoni-region to worship Tārā.
12.32
“In the morning, while worship is performed, King Naraka stands as gatekeeper; no one is able to enter my yoni-region until my worship is finished.
12.33
“Narakāsura made this a daily rule, and therefore he stopped the son of Brahmā.
12.34
“Then that sage pronounced a terrible curse. Mahādeva, listen to it; speaking of it makes me weep.
12.35
“[He said:] ‘I have done what is proper concerning the supreme place desired by Brahmā, Viṣṇu, Indra and the others. God, tell me what fault is mine.
12.36
“‘I leave you, because you have acted otherwise toward me. Today the sin of killing a Brahmin shall truly be yours.
12.37
“‘By my declaration, accomplishment will never arise in this great seat through repetition or worship.’
12.38
“Thus, cursed by the sage, I have come to you. Compassionate one, tell me what remedy I should undertake.”
12.39 — Īśvara continued:
Having repeatedly comforted her as she wept, I came to the yoni-seat and repeated Kālikā's mantra—
12.40
—to remove the curse, Queen of the gods. Having freed her from that curse, I established Kāmākhyā in the yoni-region as before.
12.41
Then supreme Māyā rejoiced greatly. Nevertheless, Great Lady, in the Kali age the Brahmin's curse will certainly bear fruit for three hundred years.
12.42 — The Goddess said:
You have told everything concerning Vasiṣṭha's former curse upon Kāmākhyā, who dwells in Kāma, in connection with Naraka.
12.43
Now I wish to hear the account of its removal. Explain how the curse is recognized and its signs, hearing which good people will always become compassionate.
12.44 — Īśvara said:
When the kingdom of the foolish ruler of the city is destroyed, Supreme Lady, from that day the Brahmin's curse begins.
12.45
Then there will be exceedingly bad conduct in Kāmarūpa, oppression of the people, disease, sorcery and many evils.
12.46
Mahāmāyā, there will be constant warfare and wickedness; gods, Dānavas and Gandharvas will continually suffer.
12.47
In the Śaka year encoded as ku-pūrva-kula-candra [reading uncertain], fierce warfare between Saumāras, Kuvācas and Yavanas—
12.48
—will arise in Kāmapīṭha, crowded with many armies. Then, defeating the Saumāra in battle, a Yavana [ruler]—
12.49
—whose name begins with ma will rule for one year.
Note: The encoded year and the gloss's printed numeral are not sufficiently secure for a historical date. Saumāra, Kuvāca and Yavana are preserved without automatic identification with modern communities.
12.50
Obtaining his assistance, the Kuvāca will enjoy his own kingdom. At the end of a year the Saumāra will defeat the Yavana and become ruler.
12.51
Great Lady, when the Śaka year encoded as kumārī-candra-śaśāṅka [uncertain reading] has passed, warfare will again arise in Kāmarūpa.
12.52
Then, allied with the Kuvāca, the Yavana will rule Kāmarūpa for twelve years, Great Lady.
12.53
[An encoded name or letter sequence follows.] Thereafter Kāmarūpa is to be pacified by the Saumāras and Kuvācas.
Note: The first half is not securely recoverable from the supplied text layer; a ruler's name is not invented.
12.54
Goddess, during the curse the Yavana, Kuvāca, Saumāra and Plava will be lords of Kāmarūpa, and no others.
12.55
In this way, Sovereign Lady, I shall explain the many signs. Everything is made clear individually, Supreme Lady.
12.56
Beloved, the forest-fire of Vasiṣṭha's austerity will subside, and trees will grow upon Mount Śālākhyā [name uncertain].
12.57
Near the city, stones will fall at Svargadvāra; Kāmākhyā's shrine will break; [there will be] conjunction with Urvaśī—
12.58
—and, Queen of the gods, the Brahmaputra's current will become thin. After sixteen [years, in an encoded Śaka expression], [rulers] will leave Saumāra and Kāmapīṭha for a place whose name is textually uncertain.
Note: The long compound of year and destination is badly recognized, and the Hindi repeats the unclear place-name. It remains unresolved rather than assigned a modern location.
12.59
Remaining there six months, all the kings skilled in warfare will go to Uttarakāla and Koṣa—
12.60
—and a great battle will occur with the Kuvāca, Yavana and Cāndra, crowded with armies, involving the three mleccha groups.
12.61
The Lauhitya will be filled with blood and the heads of horses, men and elephants: there is no doubt.
12.62
Then supreme Māyā Kāmākhyā, honoured by companies of Yoginīs, dark in colour, bearing bali, smiling—
12.63
—with lolling tongue, a garland of heads, and clothed in space, will take her place upon the mountain's summit and drink blood.
12.64
Then the Kuvāca, leaving the Yavana and [destroying or being defeated by] the Saumāra, will wage a great battle as far as the Karatoyā River.
Note: The Saumāra compound permits uncertainty of agency; the Hindi reads destruction of the Saumāras.
12.65
Remaining there ten days, they will return to their homes. Then a Brahmin will become king and make Kāmarūpa's inhabitants devoted to repetition and worship, Goddess.
12.66
After ruling in this manner for three years, a staff-bearing Brahmin king will arise, Mahāmāyā, near the yoni-region.
12.67
Then a former ruler, arriving from the northeast, [with an encoded twelve-petalled name], will unite Kāma under one umbrella and govern the whole kingdom according to dharma.
12.68
His wife will be dark-complexioned, virtuous and continually devoted to Pārvatī. That faithful woman will bear a well-disciplined royal son.
12.69
Goddess, on the twelfth day after his birth, the touchstone will appear upon Sparśācala.
12.70
Through it all inhabitants of Kāmarūpa will become wealthy. Then Vasiṣṭha's curse will be released.
12.71
Thereafter great radiance will arise in Kāmākhyā's yoni-region, and Kāmākhyā's presence will be manifest in the Kali age.
12.72
Then mantra accomplishment will arise in that yoni-region. Goddess, Kāmākhyā will bestow the fruit that has been proclaimed.
12.73
Kāmākhyā, whose lotus-feet the gods honour, will have been rendered inert and ashamed by the Brahmin's curse for three hundred years. Then everything will occur as before, Goddess.
12.74
Thus I have briefly explained the protection of Kāma. Goddess, Girijā, tell me what you wish to hear next.

COLOPHON
Thus ends Chapter 12 of the First Part of the illustrious Yoginī Tantra, in the dialogue of the Goddess and Īśvara, with the vernacular commentary.

============================================================================

YOGINĪ TANTRA — FIRST PART, CHAPTER 13
English working translation
Source: Mishra edition (1984), printed pp.155–166, PDF pp.175–186. Sanskrit primary; Hindi consulted. The narrative's caste classifications, royal genealogy and ritual claims are historical claims of the text. Unclear names and constructions are marked.

13.1 — The Goddess said:
Lord of the gods, supreme Lord, all-knowing, honoured by all: Śaṅkara, graciously tell me where you go so often.
13.2 — Īśvara said:
In the country called Koca, near the yoni-cavity, there was a virtuous, faithful ruler named Revatī, renowned among the people—
13.3
—born among the mlecchas, a beautiful Yoginī, described as possessing a firm and full bodily form.
Textual note: Shastri’s corresponding Sanskrit reads śāsikā, “female ruler,” and janaviśrutā, “renowned among the people”; these clarify the damaged Mishra extraction. The bodily description is rendered non-graphically; it does not establish an age.
13.4
In connection with begging I go to her day and night. Great Lady, I share great delight with her.
Textual note: Shastri’s parallel reads tayā me ramaṇaṃ mahat; the Mishra OCR misrecognizes this as an expression concerning death. The collated reading is followed here.
13.5 — The Goddess said:
Where was she? What austerity did she perform? How did she come to earth? Union with you is no small fruit of austerity.
13.6
Nevertheless I see great favour toward her. What has become of her now? Tell me with supreme kindness.
13.7 — Īśvara said:
Daughter of the mountain king, beloved of my life, pure-smiling one: listen, I shall briefly relate that virtuous woman's story.
13.8
She delighted with me in the rāsa play in the Ekāmra forest. That virtuous Yoginī, born from a limb of the Veda, was known as Mādhurī [name uncertain].
13.9
Daughter of the mountain, she was not satisfied with my activity; she performed severe austerity to obtain me. She fulfilled my desire in that sacred field.
13.10
Goddess, in the forest of Ekāmra, on a mountain filled with pilgrimage places, a Brahmin came begging and addressed her.
13.11
She gave him no answer, much less alms. Then the Brahmin cursed her: “Proud fool, become a mleccha!”
13.12
Having spoken thus, the Brahmin departed, and the Yoginī attained mleccha status. Therefore, if a capable person, when asked by a petitioner, does not give—
13.13
—that person meets misfortune. A capable person should act humbly. Goddess, through her austerity I became forever purchased [as her servant].
13.14
Therefore, beloved, I always share intimacy with her. Her son, born from my own body, was Velusiṃha [also read Venusimha in the gloss].
13.15
Alone, that glorious and highly intelligent [king] conquered Kāma, the Saumāras and the Gauḍas [the following numerical or regional term is uncertain], defeating all the kings.
13.16
He had many sons who governed the earth: all Kuvāca kings, righteous and irresistible in battle.
13.17
All of them remained manifest, taking refuge in Velusiṃha and yoga, Ambikā, until the end of the aeon [the exact construction is uncertain].
13.18
In time, Goddess, that Mādhavī merged into my body.
13.19
As Nandimātā is my wife, so should this Yoginī be understood; as Bhṛṅgarīṭa is my son, so is Velusiṃha my own child.
13.20
Velusiṃha too will attain supreme accomplishment at the end of the aeon.
13.21
All kings of his lineage will dwell on Kailāsa, great-souled lords of the hosts, endowed with every prosperity—
13.22
—and will always sport, Goddess, like Bhairavas, with companies of celestial maidens endowed with beauty and youth.
13.23
Whenever the Brahmin's curse again affects Kāmākhyā, he will descend and protect his Kāma; all his descendants will likewise protect Kāma.
13.24
Thus, Queen of the gods, until the aeon's end and until the curse is removed, Mahāmāyā, [they] assuredly sport through that power.
13.25
In this manner, Great Lady, in the Kali age for three hundred years, supreme Lady, mistress of life, the supreme Self—
13.26
—Kāmākhyā, Mahāmāyā, undergoes the curse. At its end everything will bear fruit. Thus, Goddess, I have explained the release from the Brahmin's curse.
13.27
Great Lady, I have fully described Kāmākhyā; nevertheless, for one under a Brahmin's curse, release is far away.
13.28
Even for one bitten by Takṣaka there is an immediate remedy; for one seized by a Brahmin's curse, remedy comes at the aeon's end.
Note: The Hindi appears to negate even an aeon-end remedy; the Sanskrit's affirmative syāt is followed here.
13.29
There is no escape from hell without its [curse's] cessation, without doubt. Beloved, all descendants are afflicted day and night—
13.30
—by many kinds of calamity for seven generations. Therefore, Goddess, one should never dishonour a Brahmin.
13.31
A Brahmin embodies all the gods, Brahmā, Viṣṇu and the others [the final words are damaged].
13.32
Wherever Brahmins eat, Hari himself eats there, and there Brahmā, Rudra, celestial beings, Ṛṣis and sages—
13.33
—the ancestors and all deities eat, without doubt. A Brahmin embodies every god; therefore do not dishonour him.
13.34
The gods always desire that Brahmins, kumārīs, Śakti, fire, revelation and cows be worshipped in the lands of ritual action.
13.35
If a single kumārī is worshipped, [that is a great] worship. I cannot express the fruit of worshipping a kumārī.
13.36
Kumārīs and Śaktis are all this moving and unmoving [universe]. If one young woman is worshipped, regarded as the self, all supreme Goddesses are worshipped, without doubt.
Note: Kumārī here denotes the recipient of nonsexual ritual honour. The text uses a distinct word, yuvatī, in the second portion.
13.37
An offering made once into fire, or a gift made once to a Brahmin, becomes ten millionfold through faith, without doubt; without faith, the result is certainly a hundredfold.
13.38
A portion of food for a cow purifies the world and cuts away all sins; likewise what is given to a kumārī or Śakti, Great Lady—
13.39
—never perishes even through innumerable aeons. Gods are the source of dharma; sacrifice and the other observances are dharma—
13.40
—and dharma is the great friend in the other world, without doubt. Goddess, after Vedic action has been performed through many births, one proceeds by dharma into the Āgamic dharma.
13.41
There one obtains liberation, destroyer of the bonds of action; then, after many births, attaining knowledge, one is released.
13.42
Through action one obtains devotion; through devotion one attains knowledge; through knowledge liberation, Great Goddess: truly, truly I say.
13.43
When the condition of knowledge arises and supreme knowledge is attained, the yogin becomes liberated: thus says the blessed Śiva.
13.44
A person does not remain fruitless from undertaking action. Therefore, Mahāmāyā, always perform action.
13.45
If by good fortune Vedic or Tantric [observance] is obtained, the discerning person should not waste time in gambling and similar amusements.
13.46
Spend it in worship of deities, repetition, sacrifice and praise. That action is twofold, divided into external and internal.
13.47
External [action] depends upon observance of rules; mental action does not in the same manner. Whether impure or pure, in whatever place—
13.48
—walking, standing, sleeping or doing anything else, beautiful-faced one, perform mental dharma: there is no defect in mental observance.
13.49
Great Lady, the sacrifice of repetition is foremost among all acts. It is my own form, without doubt.
13.50
Whoever continually abides in the sacrifice of repetition, external or internal, is liberated while living, Supreme Lady, without doubt.
13.51
Whatever Vedic and Tantric dharmas exist, Great Goddess, none amounts to a sixteenth part of the sacrifice of repetition.
13.52
Grahas, Bhūtas, Piśācas, Yakṣas, Rākṣasas, and fierce animals such as tigers, even when enraged—
13.53
—Ḍākinīs, Guhyakas, Gandharvas, creeping creatures, Dānavas, Bhairavas and the wicked dwellers of the cremation ground—
13.54
—none wishes [to approach] the repeater, Goddess, overwhelmed by fear. Beloved, sins never touch that practitioner.
13.55
This fruit is proclaimed for spoken repetition. Whispered repetition is a hundredfold greater; mental repetition is held to be a thousandfold.
13.56
Uttering the mantra by voice is called spoken repetition; repetition accompanied by only slight audibility is called whispered repetition.
13.57
What is not audible even to one's own ear is called mental [repetition]. Spoken repetition is external; mental repetition is internal.
Note: The negation in the first clause is supplied from the Hindi and the classification; the OCR omits or corrupts it.
13.58
Whispered repetition is mixed [external and internal]. Thus repetition is remembered as threefold. The deity, continually praised by repetition, becomes gracious—
13.59
—and graciously grants abundant desires and eternal liberation. Beautiful-faced one, practice, repetition and meditation are not obtained anywhere through slight austerity.
13.60
If, Queen of the gods, they are obtained through good fortune earned in many births, liberation may be attained in a single birth.
13.61
What was called knowledge of Brahman is called samādhi. Thus, Great Lady, I have briefly explained this delightful [teaching]. Great Goddess, what do you wish to hear next?

COLOPHON
Thus ends Chapter 13 of the First Part of the illustrious Yoginī Tantra, with the vernacular commentary.

============================================================================

YOGINĪ TANTRA — FIRST PART, CHAPTER 14
English working translation
Source: Mishra edition (1984), printed pp.166–181, PDF pp.186–201. Mythic origins of Plavas, Yavanas and Saumāras. The text's genealogy and judgments about communities are translated as historical narrative, not accepted as historical fact. Damaged text is marked.

14.1 — The Goddess said:
God, supreme bliss, great lord of yogins, Master and guru: graciously tell me what I wish to hear.
14.2
Eternal one, I have heard the Brahmin's story and that of your seed. Great Lord, the Plavas, Yavanas and Saumāras—
14.3
—those mlecchas who protect Kāma: how were they born? Compassionate Mahādeva, tell me.
14.4 — Īśvara said:
Śālva's sons, the Bāhlīkas, died in the Kaurava battle. Three-eyed one, no other descendant remained in that lineage.
14.5
Then a Bāhlīka woman named Kīrti, virtuous, auspicious, young, beautiful, charming, devoted to austerity and greatly intelligent—
14.6
—desiring a son, went to Kāśī and performed austerity day and night, standing before Viśveśvara at the gate of my pavilion of liberation.
Textual note: The Mishra text layer loses words in4–6 and misrecognizes Kīrti as Kīrmi. The corresponding Sanskrit passage in Shastri’s edition supplies the readable lines used here. This is an explicitly collated reading, not a silent reconstruction.
14.7
Then Bali's son Bāṇa and mighty Mahākāla were guardians of that gate, Goddess. Seeing her, [Bhairava]—
14.8
—who held authority from me, became overcome by desire: wearing a skull-garland, delighted with liquor, and assuming a madman's guise.
14.9
Assuming the guise of an ascetic, that shameless lord of passion [united with her]. Great Goddess, [a child] arose from Kīrti, radiant like bandhūka and āmalaka [comparison uncertain].
14.10
There Bhairava [was greatly delighted], and Mahāṅkuśa was born, Great Goddess, Kīrti's son from Mahākāla's seed.
14.11
Seeing Bhairava's son I felt affection; [he was] cherished continually, that great-armed Mahāṅkuśa—
14.12
—and through Kīrti's undertaking he gained a kingdom, Śāmbhavī. Śālva, destroyer of Kāmarūpa, attained kingship as Mahāṅkuśa.
14.13
Born from Kīrti's womb, devoted to Kula observance, he continually worshipped you there as in Kāśī.
14.14
Great worship of you will occur there day and night. Arising as Mahāṅkuśa, [the clause concerning an assault upon Kāśī is uncertain].
14.15
Then, bearing the name Plava, he went to the jewel pavilion. Thus, Goddess, I have told the story concerning the Plava.
14.16
Hear a little of the Yavana story. In the Tretā age there was a king named Bāhu, devoted to dharma, greatly intelligent, a great warrior of the solar lineage.
14.17
Defeating his father's enemies, he enjoyed the earth with its seven continents and supreme pleasures, but forgot yoga and meditation.
14.18
After a long time, Mahāmāyā, deluded by his own pride, [he thought], “There is now no king on earth greater than I.
14.19
“Having struck down my father's enemies, I performed his ancestral rite.” Thus arose the arrogance that destroys everything.
14.20
Consequently sin entered that ruler. A sinful king's sovereignty never endures.
14.21
Arrogance and continual warfare are never stable; these certainly constitute great sins together with their accompaniments.
14.22
Because of that disposition royal fortune departed from him. Then the foremost Haihaya and Tālajaṅgha kings appeared.
14.23
The kings took counsel, crossed the ocean, [defeated tribute arrangements or tributaries: uncertain], and reached northern Kośala.
14.24
Bāhu was defeated by them and dispossessed within a month. King Bāhu, his kingdom conquered, went to the forest with his wife.
14.25
Bāhu died in that forest, his glory entirely gone. His steadfast son Sagara possessed immense strength and prowess; he expelled the two rulers, Tālajaṅgha and Haihaya.
14.26
Such was their fate because they had insulted Vasiṣṭha. Again those two Yavana kings, fearful for their lives—
14.27
—took refuge in Vasiṣṭha, crying, “Protect us, protect us!” The Brahmin Vasiṣṭha granted those Yavanas fearlessness.
14.28
Meanwhile King Sagara, overwhelmed by anger and wishing to kill them, came to Vasiṣṭha.
14.29
Seeing him in that state, Vasiṣṭha, born of Brahmā, said to Bāhu's son Sagara, who knew dharma:
14.30
“Son of Bāhu, do not kill them; I have given them protection.” Hearing this, Bāhu's capable son stopped.
14.31
“The Brahmin's word must not be false, but my vow was made earlier. What shall I do now? A crisis has arisen.”
14.32
Having reflected, he explained everything to Vasiṣṭha: “I previously vowed to kill all those men.
14.33
“Now, hearing your word, I am confounded. What shall I do? Great sage, I cannot contradict your declaration.
14.34
“If you provide no remedy, death would be better for me.” Hearing this, Vasiṣṭha promptly answered:
14.35 — Vasiṣṭha said:
“Friend, do not despair. Hear what must be done. Shave the heads of all these enemies, exclude them from Vedic observance, and banish them far from the country.
14.36
“King, west of the Himalaya lies the Yavana country. Thus my word will stand, and your vow too.
14.37
“Cutting off a head in battle and shaving it are alike: even in the Veda these are established as equivalent.”
14.38 — Īśvara continued:
Hearing his words, Sagara did so. All those Kṣatriyas, Haihayas and Tālajaṅghas, were humiliated, degraded, and continually shaven-headed.
14.39
They took refuge in the sage Suṣeṇa, deprived of [the prescribed] conduct, and by Suṣeṇa's instruction continually practised austerity.
14.40
Great Goddess, they followed their own desires. Taking refuge in a dark disposition, they became tamasic.
14.41
Mantras have two courses, conjunction and separation. In the sattvic and rajasic [modes], Goddess, conjunction bears fruit. Conjunction with external and internal separation is also excellent; in the tamasic [mode], separation bestows external accomplishment.
Note: This technical account of saṃyoga and viyoga is compressed and not fully explained by the gloss; its application remains provisional.
14.42
The tamasic is called an external dharma concerning the other world. Through that very disposition my grace arose toward them. I gave them a boon: “Yavanas, now enjoy this desired kingdom—”
14.43
“—and, in the Kali age, at Śaka [encoded as indu-aṣṭa-nava, glossed 981], you will certainly become rulers of a holy country.”
Note: The printed date is a textual claim, not independently verified chronology.
14.44
Thus, Great Lady, auspicious one, the Yavana became lord of Kāmarūpa. Through my grace Yavana rulers joyfully enjoy the earth in the Kali age, including other holy lands crowded with kings.
14.45
Thus I have related the Yavana account, Goddess. Now hear the Saumāra story and [its] warfare.
14.46
Once the king of immortals left the divine kingdom and came to the Khāṇḍava forest with Kauśalāṅgī.
14.47
After the divine king had sported for a long time, various desires arose in him for the threefold music of song, dance and instruments.
14.48
He summoned Rambhā, Tilottamā, Kāñcī, Kuraṅgākṣī and Manoharā, commanding them to dance with Rambhā.
14.49
Then all the celestial courtesans he had chosen danced together and vigorously delighted Indra through many performances.
14.50
Kauśāṅgī too was enchanted and united with the king of gods. Their singing and dancing then aroused desire.
14.51
At that moment, Goddess, Indra, slayer of Bala, felt attraction toward the celestial courtesan Manoharā.
14.52
Seeing Indra's affection, she also set her mind upon him; overcome by desire, she faltered in dance and song.
14.53
Their mutual delight arose, and her performance faltered again.
14.54
Knowing her mind, Kauśāṅgī became overwhelmed by anger and spoke harshly: “Listen, Manoharā!
14.55
“Wicked courtesan, you desire the god who delights in me. Therefore your restless mind has disturbed our intimacy.
14.56
“Courtesan, go to earth and obtain a mortal king as husband!” Hearing that harsh curse from Kauśāṅgī's mouth, she fainted and fell at Kauśāṅgī's feet.
14.57
Deeply distressed, she lamented and repeatedly grasped her feet. Then Kauśāṅgī said, “Manoharā, after thirty-two years, when the curse is completed, you will regain your former state.
14.58
“Leaving the body in the Mandākinī, you will return to heaven.” Becoming the enchanting woman Kaṅkatī, she obtained a Dhārtarāṣṭra as husband.
14.59
When the Kauravas were slain at Kurukṣetra, a hundred women [died]. Kaṅkatī quickly fled in fear to Mount Candracūḍa.
14.60
She remained greatly distressed upon its very high summit. On the second day of her menstrual season, that celestial courtesan was afflicted by desire.
14.61
Indra, riding his chariot from Kuśadvīpa, saw the beautiful, ornamented woman and remembered the previous cause.
14.62
Making everything known to that beloved, overcome by desire, he united with her and departed. Her son was Arindama—
14.63
—born from Kaṅkatī on Mount Gandhamādana, Supreme Lady. Since Indra had taken her on the second day of her season—
14.64
—Arindama became devoted to mleccha conduct, following a hunter's occupation, fearsome and continually harming creatures—
14.65
—eating all meats, Goddess, like a Kirāta, excluded from every meritorious act and filled with all sins—
14.66
—delighting in liquor and meat, and devoted to bad conduct. Seeing such a son, Kaṅkatī was greatly distressed.
14.67
She performed exceedingly intense austerity, essence beyond essence, higher than the highest. Then the king of gods stood before her and said:
14.68 — Indra said:
“Kaṅkatī, tell me why you perform austerity. I am greatly pleased by it and shall give whatever you desire.”
14.69 — Kaṅkatī said:
“Your son has become such a person, constantly devoted to sin. Lord of the gods, I cannot bear to see it: act as you wish.
14.70
“God above gods, lord of gods, ruler of heaven: your son will truly be a sinful, lowest man, without doubt.
14.71
“Since you are lord of the deities, this son of yours is contrary [to you]. Master, do as you wish; take me quickly—
14.72
“—to serve beside you; appoint me to service. Lord of the gods, this is my boon; I have no other desire.”
14.73 — Indra said:
“Beloved, hear me: the time of your curse has passed. I shall take you away immediately; be at peace.
14.74
“A son's sin certainly destroys a lineage. Therefore, after one hundred and twenty-eight men [or generations] have passed—
14.75
“—dwelling in Saumāra, foremost kings will arise in my lineage—
14.76
“—just-minded, greatly energetic, devoted to gods and Brahmins, knowing and speaking Brahman, without doubt. All will become dear to Viṣṇu and go to Vaikuṇṭha—
14.77
“—merging into him until the dissolution of beings.”
14.78 — Īśvara continued:
Then Indra took her and went to his own dwelling. In due time the Saumāra became lord of Kāmarūpa.
14.79
The Saumāra in the east, the Kuvāca in the west, the Yavana in the south and the Plava in the north—
14.80
—thus, Great Goddess, all were protectors of Kāma. Goddess, I have told you the Saumāra story.
14.81
What else do you wish to hear that you have never heard before?

COLOPHON
Thus ends Chapter 14 of the First Part of the illustrious Yoginī Tantra, with the vernacular commentary.

============================================================================

YOGINĪ TANTRA — FIRST PART, CHAPTER 15
English working translation
Source: Mishra edition (1984), printed pp.182–194, PDF pp.202–214. Sanskrit primary; Hindi consulted. This is a mythic explanation of Kāmākhyā, the yoni-seat, Govardhana and Vṛndāvana; its claims are not independently established geographical or historical facts. Uncertain readings are marked.

15.1 — The Goddess said:
I ask you privately: tell me, who is Kāmākhyā?
15.2 — Īśvara said:
The supreme Vidyā Kālī, eternal and of the nature of Brahman, is herself Kāmākhyā, Queen of the gods, delighting in every accomplishment.
15.3 — The Goddess said:
Great Lord, how did Kālī, whose nature is Brahman, become Kāmākhyā? Graciously explain everything, moon-crested Lord.
15.4 — Īśvara said:
When the self-born Creator himself made creation, the Grandfather became filled with the fault of arrogance.
15.5
Sovereign Lady, arrogance destroys everything. Brahmā, dwelling of the world, remained possessed of that arrogance.
15.6
He forgot every former event that Kālikā had explained; the Creator was continually filled only with the thought of “I.”
15.7
Great Goddess, without understanding whose is this world, abode of all desires, or for what cause it exists, Brahmā was deluded by arrogance.
15.8
Seeing Brahmā in that condition, Supreme Lady, [Kālī] fashioned from the arrogance within his body—
15.9
—a supremely invincible Daitya named Keśī. Issuing from Brahmā's body, that exceedingly terrible Daitya—
15.10
—rushed to devour Brahmā, Goddess. Then the Grandfather fled with Viṣṇu.
15.11
Then Keśī, the great Daitya, made a city in Bhārata, renowned as Keśipura. Dwelling there, the Dānava—
15.12
—enjoyed everything, Goddess, the moving and unmoving in earth, atmosphere and heaven. “Kill Brahmā!” continually resounded through the universe.
15.13
Then Brahmā, creator of the world, free of arrogance, together with Viṣṇu praised Kālī, Mother of the worlds, destroyer of obstacles.
15.14 — Brahmā and Viṣṇu said:
“Homage to the supremely auspicious sovereign Goddess, whose self is praṇava, whose form is her own seed and the Kāma seed—
15.15
“—lovely with garlands of heads, lolling-tongued and eternal, whose form is the Māyā seed and Kūrca seed.
15.16
“We honour Kālī, sustainer of the worlds, lotus-eyed, terrible-roaring, uttering the jackals' cry, loose-haired, clothed in space.
15.17
“We bow to the Goddess Kālikā, destroyer of great obstacles; we always bow to Rudrā, sovereign of the three worlds.”
15.18 — Īśvara continued:
Thus praised by Brahmā and Viṣṇu, the Goddess suddenly spoke from the sky: “What do you desire, Grandfather? Mighty-armed Viṣṇu, tell me what you too desire.”
15.19 — Brahmā and Viṣṇu said:
“A great Asura, king of Daityas, named Keśī has arisen. He has taken the entire region that belonged to us.
15.20
“Kill that Asura now and restore us as before. Give us service at your lotus-feet: this is our request.”
15.21 — Kālī said:
“Brahmā, hear my word: your arrogance has gone. Now I am pleased; the world will return to you.
15.22
“See, your entire universe consists of Māyā and belongs to no other. Seeing your arrogance, lotus-born one, I gave you an irresistible obstacle—
15.23
“—the form of the Keśī Daitya, arising because of your arrogance. I shall destroy that obstacle: remain steady. Viṣṇu of great intelligence, do not fear; be steady.”
15.24 — Īśvara continued:
Having thus reassured Brahmā and Viṣṇu, supreme Kālī reduced the foremost Dānava, the Asura Keśī, to ashes with her hūṃ sound alone. Then she said to the Creator:
15.25 — Kālī said:
“Brahmā, a great sin has arisen from arrogance. For removing that sin, construct an excellent mountain—
15.26
“—from Keśī's ashes, filled with grasses for feeding cows. By continually eating that fodder, cows will consume your sin.”
15.27 — Īśvara continued:
Gathering the ashes of Keśī's body and sprinkling water from his ascetic vessel, the Creator made a mountain.
15.28
Neither excessively high nor low, it was covered with abundant cow-fodder. Eating its grass, cows became content and well-nourished.
15.29
Therefore the Creator gave the mountain the name Govardhana. As cows ate the fodder on that excellent mountain, auspicious one, so Brahmā's sin diminished.
15.30
The Creator's release [continued or was withheld] while Govardhana endured; then Brahmā's release occurred, Supreme Lady.
Note: The correlation tāvat/yāvat is awkward in this printing. The Hindi understands full release through Govardhana; the exact temporal assertion remains uncertain.
15.31
Likewise, if an offence arises toward one of eminent standing, its fruit may be removed in this manner [the final phrase is damaged].
15.32
Then Brahmā, creator of the world, and Viṣṇu, lord of the worlds, again praised the supreme Goddess in the same order.
15.33
Then Kālī, Mother of the worlds, said to them: “Children, what do you desire? I shall give everything. Why are you distressed?”
15.34 — Brahmā and Viṣṇu said:
“For our welfare and the world's, your exceedingly pure lotus-feet bestow great liberation—
15.35
“—Mother, invisible, secret and without form. How shall we worship those feet that grant every auspiciousness?
15.36
“Conceive a place on earth for worshipping those lotus-feet. We shall always worship them, the cause of great auspiciousness—
15.37
“—for our own and everyone's great liberation. Then what harm can Dānavas and the others do to us? Through worshipping your feet we shall certainly cross what is difficult to cross.”
15.38 — Kālī said:
“Child Mahāviṣṇu, hear this supreme and great word: the hūṃ seed by which I reduced the Dānava to ashes—
15.39
“—the great seed of the Keśī mantra—is the form of Brahman as sound. Know that seed as great radiance and the supreme state.
15.40
“After killing Keśī I established that seed in Keśipura; its radiance covers a krośa's extent down to the underworld.
15.41
“Therefore that place, certainly filled with great radiance, is worthy of worship. Go there and worship me as you desire.
15.42
“The desired fruit is obtained through exceedingly secret worship, even by gods, Dānavas, Gandharvas and others, greatly intelligent one.
15.43
“Worship me so that no one else knows. I shall always protect you from every calamity.
15.44
“This is a place of play, Viṣṇu: I have declared it to you. The power of will that I formerly gave to Viṣṇu—
15.45
“—established in Brahman, serves him in Mahālakṣmī's form. She will arise in this city as Vṛndā.
15.46
“From Vṛndā's dwelling there, that city will be called Vṛndāvana. Your play will certainly take place in that city.
15.47
“Becoming Vṛndā, the prosperity of trees, she will always dwell here. [I shall become] destroyer of Daityas and accomplish the destruction of all Daityas—
15.48
“—hear, child, this auspicious word. Where my worship was performed for slaying Keśī—
15.49
“—both of you behold my yoni-region arisen there. Know that yoni-region as arising from my radiance.
15.50
“The yoni is the source of everyone, without doubt. God, know this yoni of mine as Prakṛti.
15.51
“Lord of the gods, worship the yoni and undertake creation properly. Grandfather, no fear will arise for you anywhere from anyone.
15.52
“My presence will certainly dwell in that seat. Know my exceedingly beautiful form as its presiding deity.
15.53
“Always worship that form in Kāmākhyā's yoni-region. Whoever approaches the yoni-region and worships Kāmākhyā—
15.54
“—becomes lord of all accomplishments and rejoices here and in the other world. No fear arises anywhere from anyone.
15.55
“For your welfare and that of others the yoni-region has been established: on earth, in Bhāratavarṣa, greatly fruitful Kāmarūpa—
15.56
“—filled with nine yonis, bestowing the fruit of great liberation. Brahmā, in lovely Kāmarūpa, whose nature is nine yonis—
15.57
“—the yoni-region shines through Kāmākhyā's radiance. Yet now [you do not see it because of your] sin.”
Textual note: The Sanskrit extraction appears to say “I do not see your sin,” conflicting with verses58–59. The bracketed rendering follows the larger argument and Hindi's adjacent explanation, but requires collation.
15.58
“From your arrogance arose threefold sin: bodily, verbal and mental.
15.59
“Through that sin you do not see my yoni-seat; blinded day and night by streams of thought about that sin.
15.60
“Verbal sin certainly perishes through sight of Govardhana. Hear from me the means of pacifying the other two.
15.61
“Bring down one star from the star-world there, and by its excellent light behold the seat and perform austerity.
15.62
“Perform terrible austerity until you see that light merged into the yoni's radiance. At its conclusion the two sins—
15.63
“—subside, without doubt; make your dwelling there quickly. Through that austerity [the place/light] is seen by no one else.
15.64
“Such is the way to welfare after an offence. Through establishing the star there, [access for] other classes is prevented—
15.65
“—[yet] child, the place is continually approached by all worlds.” Having spoken thus, the supreme Self abiding in the sky ceased.
Note: The restriction and universal-access clauses in64–65 are not clearly reconciled in this edition. They are preserved as a textual difficulty.
15.66
Bowing to supremely auspicious Kālī, the Creator and Keśava, minds filled with wonder, then exercised sovereignty.
15.67
Daughter of the mountain, leader of the Kula, thus I have told the secret you asked about: the ancient, exceedingly concealed account.

COLOPHON
Thus ends Chapter 15 of the First Part of the illustrious Yoginī Tantra, with the vernacular commentary.

============================================================================

YOGINĪ TANTRA — FIRST PART, CHAPTER 16
English working translation
Source: Mishra edition (1984), printed pp.194–204, PDF pp.214–224. Sanskrit translated with Hindi consulted. This chapter gives the text's Śākta account of Tripura's destruction and praise of Kāśī. Mythic and salvific claims belong to the source.

16.1 — The Goddess said:
I have heard that former account inaccessible to everyone. Now I wish to hear how the form of Kālī arose.
16.2 — Īśvara said:
Listen, Goddess, to the supreme secret inaccessible even to Brahmā and the others, essence beyond essence, bestowing enjoyment and liberation.
16.3
Once Brahmā and Viṣṇu disputed with each other in the ocean of waters, each saying, “I am Lord, I am Lord.”
16.4
To pacify them, Great Lady, my immeasurable, supremely purifying great liṅga appeared in the ocean—
16.5
—divine, comprising knowledge and ignorance, difficult to behold and terrifying. In its midst I moved in Rudra's form, riding the bull.
16.6
Seeing that wonder, their bodies trembled with fear. Praising with many hymns, they spoke again:
16.7 — Brahmā and Viṣṇu said:
“Tell us who you are, terrible-formed one who has arisen from the ocean.”
16.8 — The Rudra-form said:
“Seeing you disputing in the ocean, I have arisen to test your lordship.”
16.9
Hearing this, Brahmā and Keśava fell silent. Through meditation Mādhava understood the concealed truth and became pleased.
16.10
Brahmā, troubled in mind, meditated and attained [some] knowledge. The lotus-seated one knew me only in Rudra's form—
16.11
—and, remaining doubtful, the Creator then said to Viṣṇu:
16.12 — Brahmā said:
“Viṣṇu, this is that [inferior] Rudra who was born from my forehead.”
Note: The adjective is damaged in the OCR; the Hindi reads the disparaging asattama, which suits the next verse.
16.13 — Īśvara continued:
Speaking thus, he mocked and reviled [me] in many ways. Goddess, I gave Brahmā a terrible obstacle—
16.14
—because of that contempt. The obstacle became an Asura radiant as the midday sun—
16.15
—the Dānava Tripura, born from my body. Great Lady, he seized all possessions of Indra and the others, and Viṣṇu's world.
16.16
That Daitya forcibly took everything except Garuḍa and Lakṣmī; he took Brahmā's lotus seat as well.
16.17
Then the gods, led by Brahmā and Viṣṇu, fled to the Himalaya. Viṣṇu said to the Creator:
16.18
“Creator, formerly you reviled Śiva in the ocean. Through that offence we are all afflicted.
16.19
“Let us again praise the supreme Lord here on the mountain. My heart is convinced that he will bring every blessing.”
16.20
Then the gods, led by Brahmā and Viṣṇu, resorted to the Himalaya and performed austerity. My favour arose toward them.
16.21
Making the earth a chariot, moon and sun its wheels, Brahmā its driver, and the Vedas its reins—
16.22
—making the gods its parts and horses, Sumeru the bow and Vāsuki the string—
16.23
—placing the entire moving and unmoving universe upon the chariot, and making Viṣṇu the arrow, I reduced Tripura to ashes.
16.24
Together with the moving and unmoving and gods and Daityas, Great Lady, I then created the world there again.
16.25
Goddess, know the place where this moving and unmoving world was reduced to ashes as the Great Cremation Ground, the cause of all dissolution.
16.26
The radiance of all the dead gods remained there, becoming [a region] of five krośas, and the radiance of the worlds likewise—
16.27
—forming through Māyā a body [called] Traipura upon its breast [construction uncertain].
Note: The Hindi instead reads five kośas, sheaths, in explaining the body. The surrounding geographical passage suggests five krośas; the ambiguity requires collation.
16.28
There, in that place of death, I praised the supreme Goddess, Mahākālī, supreme consciousness, within that radiance.
16.29
Then Kālī, the supreme kalā, appeared in the radiance. That radiance, measuring a great island, is called Kālī.
16.30
Only Mahākālī's face is apprehended in that radiance. Therefore sages celebrate the name Girimukha, Mountain-Face.
16.31
Supreme Lady, seeing it certainly gives rise to bliss. Therefore Vedic teachers praise it as Ānandakānana, the Grove of Bliss.
16.32
All that radiance became Kālī. Just as a drop entering the ocean becomes ocean—
16.33
—so the radiance of the sun and the others became Kālī's radiance.
16.34
As various waters entering the Gaṅgā all become Gaṅgā, Goddess, so that radiance, Queen of the gods.
16.35
It became wholly one; there is no distinction, Great Lady. Know all of it as nectar, divine beauty.
16.36
Goddess, I continually bear her upon my head. Therefore my state as Śaṅkara is certainly true.
16.37
Bearing Kālī, whose extent is five krośas, upon my head, I worship day and night, filled with supreme bliss.
16.38
Therefore my state as Viśveśvara is established here, without doubt. Queen of the gods, [I am] Lord of Brahmā, Viṣṇu and the others—
16.39
—he alone is Viśveśvara, no other, Supreme Lady. Filled with pure bliss, I always worship the Supreme. Then her favour arose in a bodiless voice:
16.40 — Kālī said:
“God of supreme bliss, you have delighted me. Therefore always grant bliss to those who die in Kāśī.”
16.41 — Īśvara continued:
Hearing her words, I plunged into an ocean of nectar. I impart supreme Brahman into the ears of the dying—
16.42
—always dwelling in Vārāṇasī, Goddess, absorbed in meditation. Those who die in Vārāṇasī, whether in water, on land or in the air—
16.43
—to them I impart supreme Brahman within hearing. Abandoning every good and evil action, a person instantly attains liberation in Brahman through my instruction.
16.44
Great Lady, all good and evil action becomes ashes through the yoga of knowledge obtained by Mahākālī's grace.
16.45
Whatever comes into contact with Kāśī instantly becomes Kāśī. Through merely touching Kāśī, the mass of sins perishes.
16.46
The trident-bearer burns action instantly through contact with Kālī's radiance, as fire burns a heap of cotton in a little time, auspicious one.
16.47
Thus Kāśī burns the mass of actions from human births in a single birth. What can I tell you of the store of merit that is Kāśī?
16.48
If there were a woman equal to you or a man equal to me, Goddess, they might express a little of Kāśī's fruit.
16.49
Egg-born, heat-born, sprouting and womb-born beings: all attain liberation if, through good fortune, they die in Kāśī.
16.50
Goddess, this auspicious Vārāṇasī is filled with great radiance. According to the ages, people see it in four forms.
16.51
In the Kṛta age Kāśī consists of jewels, in Tretā of gold, in Dvāpara of stone, and in Kali of auspicious earth.
16.52
There is no sacred field higher than it in the three worlds. Great Goddess, truly, truly, I tell you under oath.
16.53
Whoever wishes liberation from the road of worldly existence should dwell restrained in Kāśī, becoming like a stone [steadfast and unmoving].
16.54
That person is learned, knowledgeable and a purifier of the lineage who, Great Goddess, does not leave Kāśī even at life's end.
16.55
Whoever reaches Kāśī and abandons it is exceedingly foolish, a destroyer of lineage; such a foolish life is in vain.
16.56
If through merit accumulated over many births one obtains Kāśī, one should never leave it, even at life's end.
16.57
Whoever wishes easily to cross the ocean of worldly existence should carefully go to my city Vārāṇasī.
16.58
Annapūrṇā gives food, Sarasvatī gives knowledge, and I, always dwelling in Kāśī, bestow liberation at life's end.
16.59
Goddess, Girijā, I have explained what you asked me, supremely purifying liberation. What else do you wish to hear?

COLOPHON
Thus ends Chapter 16 of the First Part of the illustrious Yoginī Tantra, with the vernacular commentary.

============================================================================

YOGINĪ TANTRA — FIRST PART, CHAPTER 17
English working translation
Source: Mishra edition (1984), printed pp.205–215, PDF pp.225–235. Sanskrit primary; Hindi consulted. Kumārī worship here is nonsexual ritual honour to a girl as the Goddess. The caste and supernatural claims are those of the source. Unclear readings are marked.

17.1 — The Goddess said:
Ancient supreme Lord, guru of all worlds, lord of the world's highest power: tell me of Kola's overthrow.
17.2 — Īśvara said:
Listen, Goddess: I shall explain Kolāsura's overthrow, this wondrous account connected with Mahākālī.
17.3
Sin arose in immeasurably radiant Viṣṇu through a Brahmin's curse. Afflicted by that sin, the all-knowing one performed austerity.
17.4
Going near the Himalaya, he continually repeated Mahākālī's eight-syllabled Great Vidyā that destroys this sin.
17.5
At the end of ten thousand years Maheśvarī was pleased. Merely through her satisfaction, an Asura named Kola suddenly emerged from Viṣṇu's heart-lotus.
17.6
That powerful, wicked great Asura defeated all gods beginning with Indra, seized everything, took Viṣṇu's abode and Brahmā's lotus seat.
17.7
Then Viṣṇu and the other gods went to eternal Kālī and praised her devotedly, saying, “Protect us, protect us!”
17.8 — Kālī said:
“Child Viṣṇu, now, assuming a kumārī's form and entering Kola's city, I shall kill Kola together with his relations.”
17.9 — Īśvara continued:
Hearing her words, Brahmā, Viṣṇu and the other gods plunged into an ocean of bliss and danced like peacocks at thunderclouds.
17.10
Then terrible-faced Kālī assumed the form of a Brahmin girl, went to Kolāpura, approached the foremost Daitya Kolāsura and asked him for food.
17.11 — Kālī said:
“I have neither mother nor father, and no helper. Great king, I am hungry: give me food.”
17.12 — Īśvara continued:
Then Kolāsura, deluded by Māyā, compassionately took her hand and himself entered the inner palace.
17.13
He said, “I shall give you whatever food you desire. Child, sit here upon this jewel-adorned seat.”
17.14
Having said this, he repeatedly gave many kinds of food. Goddess, she ate everything and said, “Give more.”
17.15
Again he gave much more, and she ate it. When she said, “I am not satisfied,” the great Asura replied:
17.16
“Child, do whatever will satisfy you.” Hearing this, Kālī in the child's form—
17.17
—in a moment devoured the treasury, horses, elephants, chariots, army and relations, and mighty Kola himself.
17.18
As Kālarudra destroys the three worlds in a moment at dissolution, auspicious one, Kālī emptied Kolāpura in a moment.
17.19
Seeing the Asuras destroyed, the gods headed by Viṣṇu continually showered flowers and danced greatly.
17.20
Gods, Gandharvas and Kinnaras sang exquisitely; Vidyādharīs, gods' wives and Kinnarī women [celebrated] all around.
17.21
They worshipped that kumārī with flowers from Nandana. All worlds worshipped the kumārī day after day.
17.22
Then the kumārī, whose body is Brahman, disappeared, Goddess. Thus I have explained Kolāsura's destruction.
17.23
A Brahmin's curse is irresistible; do not provoke it even inadvertently. Beloved, always know the Brahmins' speech as a thunderbolt.
17.24
Therefore, learned or unlearned, a Brahmin should always be honoured. Goddess, when a Brahmin is satisfied, we gods are always satisfied.
17.25
When a Brahmin is displeased, Goddess, we too are displeased.
17.26
Even if a Brahmin performs a hundred improper deeds, one seeking one's own welfare should nevertheless not abandon him—
17.27
—nor ever dishonour him, foremost divine one. The Brahmin is the self of every god, equal to the radiance of liberation.
17.28
That Kālī assumed a kumārī's form without birth [or childbirth]. From then on, Queen of the gods, the gods have worshipped the kumārī.
17.29
Brahmā, Viṣṇu, Maheśa and the others always worship the kumārī, as do all other beings within the universe.
17.30
Beautiful one, I cannot express the fruit of kumārī worship even with countless thousands of tongues and countless hundreds of mouths.
17.31
Therefore worship a girl born in any social group. Auspicious one, no distinction of caste should be made in kumārī worship.
17.32
Through making caste distinctions, Great Lady, one does not return from hell. A mantra practitioner who harbours doubt certainly becomes sinful.
17.33
Therefore worship her with great devotion, regarding her as the Goddess. The kumārī is the form of all Vidyās, without doubt.
17.34
When a single girl is worshipped, everything is worshipped. If by good fortune, Goddess, [one finds] a girl born in a courtesan's family—
17.35
—beloved, the practitioner should carefully and joyfully worship her with gold, silver and the like, even with all possessions.
17.36
Then great accomplishment arises for the practitioner, without doubt. Accomplished in the great attainment, that person is Śrī Sadāśiva himself.
17.37
I shall tell the signs; hear, sweet-speaking one. Great Lady, that person's body becomes golden, and endowed with all accomplishments one sports like Bhairava.
17.38
Passage in heaven, the mortal world and the underworld is certain for that person. Whatever comes into the mind suddenly occurs.
17.39
By assuming a multiplicity of bodies one becomes present everywhere, unobstructed in every place, like Purandara, auspicious one.
17.40
Women of the gods, Dānavas, Gandharvas, Nāgas and Kinnaras, Vidyādharīs and royal women serve that person day and night.
17.41
In the end that person attains supreme, excellent nirvāṇa. During kumārī worship the practitioner attains Śivahood.
17.42
The country where a kumārī is worshipped purifies the earth and becomes exceedingly meritorious for five krośas around.
17.43
Supreme Lady, wherever kumārī worship is performed, a great light manifests visibly on the earth of Bhārata.
17.44
A king named Viśvambhara of the Caitra lineage worshipped a dark-complexioned kumārī named Kāñcī, born in a courtesan's family and endowed with every auspicious sign.
17.45
At the time of worship, Great Goddess, Kāñcī became radiant. Enveloped by that mass of light, the king attained liberation.
17.46
Kāñcī's radiance was not wholly [manifest or complete: uncertain]; becoming eternally luminous, she continually receives worship at that place.
17.47
There arose the greatly fruitful city called Kāñcī. Know it as auspicious, bestowing liberation, with an extent of five krośas.
17.48
Whatever household business or other activity is performed there becomes worship of her: this is wondrous, daughter of the mountain.
17.49
Therefore, Goddess, auspicious Kāñcī is equal to Vārāṇasī. Likewise the great-souled Kāmpilya formerly worshipped a girl.
17.50
Beautiful-faced one, she formerly arose in Kāmpilya city; even today she is seen in the world, abiding in a stone form.
Note: The text layer resembles śivarūpa, but the Hindi supports śilārūpa, “stone form.” This is a contextual correction.
17.51
[That] dwelling is exceedingly meritorious, containing all pilgrimage places, accompanied by every sacrifice and surrounded by great sages.
17.52
Excellent Kāmpilya city is filled with every wonder. Great Goddess, those who dwell in auspicious Kāmpilya—
17.53
—enjoy excellent pleasures and abundant satisfaction here, filled with every prosperity; at the end, through the Goddess's grace, they pass beyond Viṣṇu's world and attain her abode.
Note: The Sanskrit reads Viṣṇu's world; the Hindi gloss instead says Brahmā's world.
17.54
If someone still possesses desire, that person goes to supreme Vaikuṇṭha or to my world, Great Lady, to my jewel dwelling.
17.55
Auspicious one, today I have told you a little of the kumārī's story. Mahāmāyā, what else should be explained?

COLOPHON
Thus ends Chapter 17 of the First Part of the illustrious Yoginī Tantra, with the vernacular commentary.

============================================================================

YOGINĪ TANTRA — FIRST PART, CHAPTER 18
English working translation
Source: Mishra edition (1984), printed pp.215–224, PDF pp.235–244. Sanskrit primary; Hindi consulted. Account of Kahola and praise of the Gaṅgā. The claims about rebirth, caste, sin and liberation belong to the historical source. Unclear readings are marked.

18.1 — The Goddess said:
I have heard the ancient kumārī story desired by the gods. Now I wish to hear the supreme story of Kahola.
18.2
Mahādeva, graciously explain it: beneficial to all worlds, fulfilling the heart's purpose of yogins.
18.3 — Īśvara said:
Endowed with the nature of the Vedas, Vedānta, Vedāṅgas and all scriptures, lord of all yogins, purified by pilgrimage places and free of all sin—
18.4
—accomplished in every Vidyā and mantra, foremost Ṛṣi, honoured by all, that excellent sage wandered the earth.
18.5
A great anxiety arose in him and his mind became exceedingly troubled. Formerly, auspicious one, he had shone like a thousand suns—
18.6
—but through profound anxiety he became like a [small] lamp. Then the great sage heard a voice arising from the sky:
18.7
“Kahola, go east to the god Śaṅkara, giver of welfare. He is guru of all beings and will accomplish everything for you.”
18.8
Hearing that heavenly voice, flooded with supreme bliss, Kahola came and told me the entire account.
18.9
Then I gave him the auspicious, conscious Vidyā Kālī, the supreme kalā, [nineteen-syllabled: reading uncertain], fostering all knowledge.
Note: The syllable-count compound is damaged as na-candrākṣarī in the OCR. The exact count is provisional and should not be used as a verified mantra identification.
18.10
I gave him the supreme observance approved by the illustrious Āgama, and said to him: “Child Kahola, listen.
18.11
“Through this observance, approach Kālī clothed in space; contemplate and worship her, destroyer of all ignorance.
18.12
“Then your doubts will be destroyed, lord of beings.” Thus commanded, the Veda-knowing sage Kahola went to Kāśī and joyfully worshipped Kālī with the fivefold observance.
18.13
Then the doubts and obstacles in the heart of highly intelligent Kahola, embodiment of the Veda, fled day by day.
18.14
Mahākālī's auspicious grace arose toward that sage. Endowed with her grace, excellent sage Kahola—
18.15
—regarded himself as Kālikā, eternal Brahman, freed by knowledge from Māyā, supreme and nondual, auspicious one.
18.16
That excellent sage became free from every Māyā, and this entire moving and unmoving world became Brahman [to his vision].
18.17
He no longer knew his own body and other distinctions. Through that knowledge of Brahman, Great Lady, the great-souled sage's bodily actions and the rest became ashes.
18.18
Through Mahākālī's grace Kahola became Brahman. Engaged in all transgressive observances, he shone forth.
Note: Atyācāra is retained in its transgressive sense; a moral judgment supplied by the translator is not intended.
18.19
Thus honoured by Kahola, Mahākālī merged that sage into her own causal body.
18.20
Whatever disposition a practitioner adopts in worshipping Kālī, through Mahākālī's grace that person quickly obtains [the corresponding state].
18.21 — The Goddess said:
I have heard the ancient, wondrous Kahola story, [wondrous] even to gods, Asuras, great sages and self-controlled Ṛṣis.
18.22
Now, Lord, I wish to hear of Gaṅgā, purifier of the three worlds. Tell me through obtaining which Vidyā she became [such].
18.23 — Īśvara said:
Listen, Goddess: I shall explain what you ask. Through hearing it every sin meets destruction, and not otherwise.
18.24
First I shall explain the mantra named “Purifier of the Three Worlds,” of the Great Goddess Mahākālī, spoken by Mahādeva.
18.25
Extract her own seed, then two vocative expressions, then her own seed again: know that renowned Vidyā.
Note: This is encoded mantra construction; the unexpressed names or syllables are not conjecturally expanded.
18.26
Worshipping this Vidyā, Gaṅgā, purifier of the three worlds, dwelt upon my matted locks and repeated it day and night.
18.27
Thus purifying Gaṅgā bestows liberation on every embodied being; through the power of the accomplished mantra, she is increased by Kālī's radiance.
18.28
Therefore she became purifying and liberation-giving, Sadāśiva [address as printed]. For that reason the Vidyā is honoured at all pilgrimage places.
18.29
Queen of the gods, what can I say of Gaṅgā's greatness? Merely remembering her name, sinners partake of liberation.
18.30
Whoever says “Gaṅgā, Gaṅgā,” even a sinner among sinners, abandons all sins and goes to Viṣṇu's city.
18.31
Whatever sins are proclaimed, Great Lady, those without expiation and those for which expiation is prescribed—
18.32
—all perish through sprinkling with Gaṅgā water. Whatever water flows into Gaṅgā from a town or city—
18.33
—becomes Gaṅgā itself through the mantra's greatness, auspicious one.
18.34
The country where Gaṅgā dwells is a vessel of merit.
18.35
A sacred, purifying region of two yojanas is proclaimed. Whatever action is done there is done in Gaṅgā, without doubt.
18.36
Goddess, whatever is done in Gaṅgā has imperishable fruit, whether complete in its parts or deficient, beloved of Śambhu.
18.37
All beings dwelling there have come from the divine world. Enjoying varied pleasures and continually performing good deeds, they easily reach the supremely rare abode—
18.38
—where people do not sorrow, praised by gods and Ṛṣis. Whoever virtuously gives up life at Gaṅgā—
18.39
—attains liberation through knowledge, or Vaikuṇṭha in its absence. Great Lady, even the heavenly worlds are freed from sin through Gaṅgā.
18.40
Whoever sets out toward Gaṅgā and loses life on the road, even if sinful, goes to Viṣṇu's richly blessed world.
18.41
Whoever gives up life on her bank, whether rightly or otherwise, attains heaven endowed with all blessings.
18.42
For as long as the dead person's bones remain in Gaṅgā, that person is honoured in heaven for thousands of years.
18.43
Whoever dies anywhere in contact with Gaṅgā water is freed from all sins and honoured in Viṣṇu's world.
18.44
With concentrated mind, give ancestral food-balls on Gaṅgā's bank, four hand-lengths [from the stream]. Having released the ancestors, a person dwells in Viṣṇu's world.
18.45
Goddess, when a meritorious person performs ancestral libation in Gaṅgā, the ancestors certainly receive great satisfaction for a hundred years.
18.46
The same is declared for Ṛṣis and deities. Whether by day, at night, at twilight or in the great midnight—
18.47
—perform bathing, gifts, austerity, fire-offering, libation and worship in Gaṅgā, auspicious one; do not make distinctions of time.
18.48
If, making distinctions of time, one abandons the rite, auspicious one, one subsequently becomes an immobile being in the forest—
18.49
—and perishes by flames of forest fire or another cause. Thereafter, Supreme Lady, one becomes a perpetually suffering Caṇḍāla—
18.50
—for seven births; thereafter a washerman for three births, Great Lady, and then in Śūdra births—
18.51
—for ten births, Great Lady; thereafter one attains Vaiśya status, then Brahmin status and a meritorious destination.
Note: The Hindi adds further numbers and Kṣatriya births not clearly present in the printed Sanskrit excerpt; those additions are not inserted into this translation.
18.52
Supreme Lady, whoever steals anything at Gaṅgā has no escape from the hell Raurava, dark with delusion—
18.53
—until the dissolution of beings, Queen of the gods. Whoever accepts gifts on Gaṅgā's bank or in Gaṅgā becomes a dog-cooker for ten births, beloved.
Note: Śvapaca, “dog-cooker,” is a historical derogatory caste designation, not the translator's terminology for a present community.
18.54
Then, afflicted by poverty, that person wanders the earth for seven births, Great Goddess; at their end release is attained.
18.55
Thus, Great Lady, I have explained the excellent greatness of Kālikā's mantra as the cause of Gaṅgā's greatness.

COLOPHON
Thus ends Chapter 18 of the First Part of the illustrious Yoginī Tantra, with the vernacular commentary.

============================================================================

YOGINĪ TANTRA — FIRST PART, CHAPTER 19
English working translation
Source: Mishra edition (1984), printed pp.225–238, PDF pp.245–258. Sanskrit primary; Hindi consulted. Account of Karāla Bhairava's initiation as Krodhavaktra. Ritual claims and terms belong to the source; uncertain readings are marked. Seed syllables encoded but not stated are not invented.

19.1 — The Goddess said:
God of supreme bliss, ocean of compassion, who besides you in the endless universe rescues those fallen into the boundless, terrible ocean of worldly existence?
19.2
You are certainly guru of all beings, beginning with Brahmā. Therefore, Lord, I ask you: speak with supreme kindness.
19.3
Lord of the world, I have heard everything issuing from your lotus-mouth. Now I wish to hear what has come into my mind.
19.4
By taking refuge in which Vidyā did Karāla Bhairava himself become Krodhavaktra Bhairava, giver of fear to all beings—
19.5
—from whom gods, Asuras and others, distressed by fear, abandon their own glory and strength and flee hither and thither?
19.6
Lord clothed in space, I wish to hear that Vidyā. Explain it.
19.7 — Īśvara said:
That Great Vidyā is exceedingly secret, more secret than the secret, the whole treasure of Brahmā, Viṣṇu, Śiva and the others, for as long as life endures.
19.8
Supreme Lady, I cannot fully express that Vidyā. Only its name is stated; know it, Bhairavī of the Kula.
19.9
Supreme Lady, [she is] Sundarī, giver of boons, queen of Vidyās, Ghorakālī, Aniruddhasarasvatī.
19.10
